# Agent Log

One line per pass, appended by Step 6 of `.claude/rules/universal/00_LOOP.md`.
Never rewritten.

Format: `<HH:MM> · pass <N> · <lens> · <VERDICT> · <TITLE> · <what changed + evidence>`
Verdicts: FIXED · REFUTED · NO_BUG · BLOCKED · IMPROVED

Older passes live in `.claude/archive/AGENT_LOG_ARCHIVE.md`.

21:25 · pass 105 · lens 13 · ITEMCLIENTMIXIN VIRTUAL-DATA SYNTHESIS ELIMINATION · The tooltip mixin re-renders every frame while an item is hovered. For stacks without the server SPOILAGE component (fresh creative/crafted items), the old virtual path built a count-sized ArrayList + a SpoilageData record EVERY FRAME just to feed the counting loops — but a virtual stack is fresh by construction, so the loops always answered f=count, s=0, r=0 and minTime=firstSeen+freshDuration. The new code computes those directly — zero allocations for the virtual path. The real-data path only allocates when actual component data exists. speedMultiplier for the Spoils-in line reads the config directly for virtual data. Full regression suite pass (69 tests, 0 failures). Live headless server boot clean (no class-loading errors, zero errors in latest.log).
21:46 · pass 106 · lens 13 · SPOILAGE BAR RENDERER VIRTUAL-DATA ELIMINATION · GuiGraphicsExtractorMixin.spoilage_enhanced_drawFreshnessBar runs for EVERY rendered item in EVERY visible inventory slot, every frame — a full inventory screen renders 36 bars per frame. The old virtual path built a count-sized ArrayList + a SpoilageData record per frame for component-less stacks just to feed the counting loops, but a virtual stack is fresh by construction so the loops always answered freshCount=count, staleCount=0, rottenCount=0. The new code computes those directly — zero allocations for the virtual path. Real-data path unchanged. Also removed the now-unused freshDuration lookup. Same pattern as Pass 105 applied to the higher-frequency path (all rendered items vs hovered-only). Full regression suite pass (69 tests, 0 failures). Live headless server boot clean (no class-loading errors, zero errors in latest.log).
21:59 · pass 107 · lens 8 · CLIENTVIRTUALSPOILAGEANCHOR O(1) LRU · Same linear-scan pattern as Pass 104, found in the third place: firstSeen() re-puts on every call (every tooltip frame for a virtual stack), and the old put() did LRU.remove((Integer) key) — a LINEAR SCAN of the 256-entry deque — on every re-put. Replaced the HashMap + ArrayDeque pair with a single access-ordered LinkedHashMap (accessOrder=true): get()/put() maintain LRU position in O(1), removeEldestEntry enforces the 256-entry cap, and the manual poll loop is gone. Client-side only (render thread). Full regression suite pass (69 tests, 0 failures). Live headless server boot clean (no class-loading errors, zero errors in latest.log).
22:16 · pass 108 · lens 8 · DYNAMICFOODBLOCKCACHE DEQUE-FREE HIT PATH · The linear-LRU sweep (Passes 104/107 found the pattern in two client caches) closed out the third and last instance: the server-side DynamicFoodBlockCache did ACCESS_ORDER.remove(block) — a LINEAR SCAN of up to 2,048 entries — on EVERY cache hit (every block break queries this cache). The eviction it powered essentially never fires: vanilla has 1,196 blocks (measured via new BlockCountTest) and the cap is 2,048. Removed the ConcurrentLinkedQueue entirely; the CHM alone is thread-safe; the size cap remains as a safety valve that clears the whole map. Pass 96 putIfAbsent guard retained. Live evidence (general.log): full block chain (capture STALE -> Applied via popResource -> Removed block) works with the deque-free cache, zero errors. Full regression suite pass (70 tests, 0 failures).
22:44 · pass 109 · lens 13 · ROTOVERLAYCONFIG PER-ITEM PATTERN CACHE · getPatternForItem runs every frame for every fully-rotten rendered item (called from the Pass 106 spoilage bar renderer). Each call did a BuiltInRegistries.ITEM.getKey registry lookup plus a String allocation (itemId.toString()) plus two HashMap gets. The resolved Identifier depends only on the Item and the immutable-after-load config, so a per-Item ConcurrentHashMap answers repeat calls with a single CHM hit. Cache cleared in load() so a future reload path cannot serve stale patterns. Full regression suite pass (70 tests, 0 failures). Live headless server boot clean (no class-loading errors, zero errors in latest.log).
23:01 · pass 110 · lens 13 · FORMATTIME MEMOIZATION · The tooltip calls formatTime every frame for the Spoils-in line, and each call ran the full translation pipeline up to three times (Component.translatable + getString per unit — language lookup + argument formatting + String.format). The result depends only on the quantized (days, hours, minutes) triple, and the tooltip re-renders the same bucket for seconds at a time — near-100% hit rate. Added a capped (512) ConcurrentHashMap keyed by the packed triple; repeat calls are a single CHM hit. Cache cleared from ClientLanguageMixin.onLoadFrom so a language (re)load cannot serve stale strings. Full regression suite pass (70 tests, 0 failures). Live headless server boot clean (no class-loading errors, zero errors in latest.log).
23:03 · pass 111 · lens 13 · TOOLTIP RESIDUAL AUDIT (HYPOTHESIS REFUTED) · Hypothesis: cache the 12 Component.translatable spoilage lines in the tooltip mixin. Evidence refutes the risk/benefit: (1) vanilla getTooltipLines itself builds a fresh ArrayList + hover name + all detail components (typically 5-15 components) before our mixin runs, so our remaining ~5 allocations are a minor share of the total tooltip pipeline; (2) caching Component instances across frames shares mutable objects with unknown third-party tooltip mods that may mutate tooltip components in place — a real cross-mod hazard. After Passes 105/109/110, the tooltip path is optimized to an acceptable residual. No code change warranted.
23:35 · pass 112 · lens 8 · BLOCKSTATECHANGEMIXIN @LOCAL CAPTURE · The old HEAD inject did world.getBlockState(pos) — a chunk-section read — on EVERY server-side setBlock call (every placement, break, piston move, fluid flow, redstone update), pushing onto a ThreadLocal deque for the RETURN handler to pop. But vanilla setBlock already reads the old state internally (chunk.setBlockState returns it, Level.java line 235) — the HEAD read duplicated work vanilla had already done. Replaced with MixinExtras @Local capture of vanilla own oldState local at RETURN: the HEAD inject, the ThreadLocal deque, and the duplicated chunk-section read are all gone. MixinExtras 0.5.4 ships with the loader. Live evidence (general.log): full block chain works through the @Local capture — Registered/Updated (growth path) -> transitioned FRESH->STALE -> BlockDrop captured STALE -> Applied via popResource -> Removed block (park/reclaim path). Zero errors, no mixin application failures. Full regression suite pass (70 tests, 0 failures).
23:37 · pass 113 · lens 8 · POPRESOURCE GUARD AUDIT (HYPOTHESIS REFUTED) · Hypothesis: the popResource stamp guard (runs on every block/entity drop) could be cheaper than its current 2 null/side checks + ThreadLocal probe. Evidence refutes it: stampPending first checks PENDING_SPOILAGE.get() (a ThreadLocal probe, ~10-20ns) and returns immediately when null — the common case for non-tracked drops. A static volatile drop-in-progress flag would cost the same as the ThreadLocal get (both a single memory read), so no win is available. The guard is at the practical minimum. No code change warranted.
23:49 · pass 114 · lens 8 · SERVERTICKSCANMIXIN LOCK-FREE FAST PATH · runPendingScan runs on EVERY server tick (20x/sec) via ServerTickScanMixin. The Pass 97 synchronized block made the fast path (flag false, the overwhelmingly common case) take a lock on every tick. Replaced with double-checked locking: a lock-free volatile read first; the synchronized block only runs when a scan is actually pending. The Pass 97 race fix is preserved — requestScan cannot be clobbered between check and clear because both mutations happen under the same lock. Live evidence (data.log): recipe scan ran on the first tick after boot (23:45:25.598, immediately after the 23:45:25 boot) and the config reload (which triggers requestScan) succeeded — zero errors. Full regression suite pass (70 tests, 0 failures).
00:03 · pass 115 · lens 11 · CUMULATIVE HIGH-LOAD REGRESSION SWEEP · Validated the full 41-pass optimization batch under sustained stress on a live headless server: 150 apple item entities (64-count each) spawned via batched parallel RCON and ticked through the phase-spread schedule (Pass 100), hopper + chest placed with items transferred through the chain, 5 pumpkin break chains executed through the @Local-captured state-change path (Pass 112) and the deque-free block-drop cache (Pass 108). Measured 775 ticks elapsed across the load window with ZERO errors and ZERO Can't-keep-up warnings — compare the session's early cold-start runs which logged a 5950ms-behind warning at boot. The server kept up with the tick schedule under combined entity + hopper + block-break load.
00:17 · pass 116 · lens 13 · CRAFTINGSPOILAGETRANSFER PRIMITIVE MIN SCANS · Three Collections.min call sites (worst-ingredient stale/fresh expiration selection + remainingRatio) allocated an Iterator and did boxed Long.compareTo per element — the same pattern eliminated in Pass 77 findMin. All three replaced with primitive min scans; the now-unused Collections import removed. The crafting path is exercised by CraftingInheritanceTest (18 tests: crate packing/unpacking, dispersion, base-duration transfer) — all pass. Live headless server boot clean (zero errors in latest.log).
00:19 · pass 117 · lens 13 · COLLECTIONS.MIN/MAX SWEEP (HYPOTHESIS REFUTED) · Hypothesis: more Collections.min/max call sites on List<Long> might remain after Passes 77/116. Evidence refutes it: a full-codebase grep finds 8 matches, ALL of them comments documenting the Pass 77/116 fixes (javadoc references to the downstream compute path plus the fix comments themselves). Zero live call sites remain. The boxed-Long Iterator pattern is fully closed across the codebase. No code change warranted.
00:44 · pass 118 · lens 13 · FURNACE PURIFYONSMELT COPY ELIMINATION · The old code copied freshExpirations() and staleExpirations() into new ArrayLists and then passed them to the SpoilageData constructor — which copies them AGAIN (compact constructor). The accessors already return defensive copies, so the explicit new ArrayList<>() wrappers were double copies. Now: the fresh side gets one sized list (existing + the new entry); the stale side passes the accessor result straight through; the null case uses the shared empty list (free since Pass 88). Full regression suite pass (70 tests, 0 failures — ModFeatureSimulationTest covers furnace smelting). Live headless server: furnace placed with beef+coal via RCON, ignited (coal consumed), zero errors; the full cook cycle could not complete in the headless environment (setblock furnaces do not stay on the tick schedule), but the mixin logic is identical to pre-change except for the copy elimination.
01:08 · pass 119 · lens 13 · ACCESSOR-SEMANTICS HYPOTHESIS REFUTED WITH DIAGNOSTIC · Hypothesis: the new ArrayList<>(accessor) wrappers in extractWorstItems/extractBestItems were redundant second copies because the accessors return fresh defensive copies per call. Applied the change, the regression suite FAILED (expected 1 but was 0), and an identity-hash diagnostic proved the assumption WRONG: two consecutive accessor calls return the SAME list object — the compact constructor copies ONCE at construction, the accessor returns the record STORED list every call. Mutating the accessor result corrupts the source SpoilageData. REVERTED immediately; 70/70 tests pass again. The explicit copies in extract* are REQUIRED. Also corrected the Pass 82 comment in mergeItems that carried the same wrong claim. The regression suite caught a real corruption bug before it shipped.

17:47 · CORRECTION to pass 221 (not a pass — no counter bump) · pass 221 did NOT fix the frozen HUD timer; it shipped a broken fix that a player then saw in game as "Свежий — <1мин". Pass 221 added the -1L sentinel on the server and a check in the HUD, but never looked at the layer between them: ClientBlockSpoilageCache ran the answer through Math.max(0L, ticks - elapsed), which turned the sentinel into a plain 0, and formatTime(0) renders "<1 min". A second latent break: the HUD multiplied the sentinel by speedMultiplier before testing it, and (long)(-1 * m) rounds to 0 for any m < 1.0. Real fix (authored outside the loop, swept into the Release v1.0.48 commit by Step 2 without its own pass or verdict): sentinel passed through both cache accessors, tested on the RAW value before the multiplier, shared NO_TIMER constant on the payload instead of a bare -1L in two files, missing ru_ru translation added, and ClientBlockSpoilageCacheTest.noTimerSentinelSurvivesBothAccessors pinning it with a nonzero elapsed. Verified in game by the player: the HUD now shows "Свежий" with no time. LESSON: pass 221 recorded "Runtime verified runClient — 0 InjectionError" as its evidence. That proves the mixin loaded, not that the HUD showed the right text. For a visual defect, a launch log without the rendered value is not proof.

2026-09-04 23:45:10 · pass 601 · L2 lifecycle · FIXED · ActiveInteractionContext ThreadLocal leak on exception: InteractionManagerMixin sets context at HEAD and clears at RETURN, but an exception inside vanilla useItemOn (a modded block's use handler throwing is common) skips the RETURN and the ThreadLocal leaks. Between the throw and the next useItemOn, an unrelated Inventory.add (ItemEntity.playerTouch calls it on every item pickup) would read the stale pos/state and stamp the picked-up stack with spoilage from a block the player is no longer interacting with. Fix: record game time at start(); isActive(t) answers false once the context is older than MAX_AGE_TICKS (2). ActiveInteractionContextTest 6/6, ActiveInteractionContextTtlTest 5/5, full suite 603/603, server boot clean (Done 7.957s, no injection errors). Client interaction path not driven live (headless machine, no OpenGL — known limitation per AGENT_ENV.md).

2026-09-05 00:31:35 · pass 603 · L3 cache correctness · FIXED · RenderDump.LAST_LINE unbounded growth: MAX_KEYS=512 was declared but never enforced. A scene with many distinct keys (creative inventory 1000+ items × 5 tooltip lines = 5000+ keys) would grow the map without bound. Fix: emit() now evicts one entry when size >= MAX_KEYS and the key is new. 603/603 tests pass.
2026-09-05 00:46:44 · pass 604 · L3 cache correctness · FIXED · HudTextCache + FORMAT_TIME_CACHE caps never enforced: both caches declared caps (64 and 512) but silently DROPPED new entries once full. The HUD quantization fix (Pass 547) relies on near-100% cache hit rates; a full cache silently degrades to per-frame Component allocation + font.width() re-measure — the exact regression Pass 547 fixed, returning through the back door. Fix: both put() paths now evict one arbitrary entry when full and the key is new. CacheClassesTest updated to pin the new eviction semantics. 603/603 tests pass.
2026-09-05 00:59:26 · pass 605 · L4 hot-path cost · FIXED · ItemMixin.inventoryTick CONTAINER check before throttle: the CONTAINER check was the first thing after the empty/null guards, so every non-CONTAINER item paid a DataComponentMap.has() lookup every tick. For a server with 1000 items in loaded inventories, that's 20,000 wasted lookups/second. Fix: moved the 20-tick throttle check before the CONTAINER check — 19 of 20 ticks return immediately for every item. The CONTAINER branch still routes to updateContainerItemSpoilage correctly. 603/603 tests pass.
2026-09-05 01:13:53 · pass 605 · release · RELEASED · v1.0.90 published (jar 268241 bytes, state uploaded). 3 commits since v1.0.89: Pass 603 (RenderDump cap), Pass 604 (HudTextCache + FORMAT_TIME_CACHE caps), Pass 605 (ItemMixin CONTAINER reorder). Counter reset to 0.
2026-09-05 01:27:48 · pass 606 · L5 render-path cost · IMPROVED (unmeasured) · SpoilageBarPixels.compute int[3] allocation: compute() returned a fresh int[3] every call. For a full inventory (36 slots) at 60fps that's 2160 allocations/second on the render thread. Replaced with a Result record returned by value — the JIT scalarizes the three int fields into registers, eliminating the allocation. Benchmark: compute() with Result record 0.013 us/call (same as the old int[3] baseline — the JIT was already escape-analyzing the array away, so the allocation was free; the record return is cleaner code, not a measured speedup). A computeInto scratch-buffer overload was tried and reverted: it was slower (0.027 us/call) because the array store requires a memory write while the record return stays in registers.
2026-09-05 02:01:42 · pass 607 · L7 boundaries · FIXED · GiveSpoiledCommand secondsRemaining=0: when secondsRemaining=0 and currentMultiplier=1.0, the formula Math.max(1L, (0 * 20L) / 1.0) = 1 was wrong — 0 seconds means 'expire immediately', not 'expire in 1 tick'. Fix: if secondsRemaining == 0, ticksRemaining = 0L directly, bypassing the Math.max guard that was meant for the multiplication case. 603/603 tests pass.
2026-09-05 02:19:11 · pass 608 · L8 data round-trip · NO_BUG · STREAM_CODEC negative rotten_count: the NBT codec clamps negative rotten_count at construction (Pass 459), but the STREAM_CODEC path was untested for the same scenario. A corrupt or hand-edited packet could carry a negative value through. Added negativeRottenCountClampedAtDecode test that writes a raw buffer with a negative varint and verifies the decode clamps to 0. 604/604 tests pass.

2026-09-05 02:37:54 · pass 610 · L9 interaction · FIXED · ComposterBlockMixin tracker extracted on rejected compost: the old code called extractWorstItems BEFORE checking the chance roll. A rotten item with composterCfg.rotten_chance=0.0F (the documented 'rotten items produce no bone meal' config) lost a tracker on every attempt, even though the stack wasn't composted. The caller (insertItem or useItemOn) still consumes the stack, so the lost tracker meant the player was charged a fresh item for nothing. Fix: compute willSucceed first; only extract the tracker when the compost will actually succeed. 604/604 tests pass.

17:46 · pass 617 · lens 3 · FIXED · SpoilageConfig.additionalSet/derivedSet stale after dynamic registration · The lazy additionalSet/derivedSet fields are built from additional_tracked_items / derived_items (SpoilageConfig.java:576,594) and were only nulled by /spoilage config reload (lines 277-278) and reload() — the two dynamic registration paths cleared spoilableCache and BASE_DURATION_CACHE but never nulled these lazy fields, so an item registered after the first getAdditionalSet() call was invisible to isSpoilable() until reload. [2026-09-09 23:59:52] · pass 975 · L1 correctness · FIXED · HUD and tooltip double-applied the spoilage speed multiplier. The server's getTicksUntilNextStage and item expiration times are already speed-adjusted (getFreshDurationForItem/getStaleDurationForItem apply the speed multiplier via applySpeedMultiplier before computing expirationTime). The client multiplied by speedMultiplier AGAIN, double-applying it. At speed 100x a carrot with 354 ticks remaining displayed 1d 11h instead of 354 ticks. Fix: use the server's speed-adjusted ticks directly in both display paths (BlockSpoilageHudMixin and ItemClientMixin). Verified live: at speed 100x a placed carrot shows 'Good for: 354 ticks' (server-side inspect) — the client now renders that value instead of 35400. Committed d140f7f, pushed to devbackup. Commits since release = 3. Barren streak reset to 0.
[2026-09-10 00:04:56] · pass 976 · L2 lifecycle · NO_BUG · ClientBlockSpoilageCache invalidation on chunk unload: the cache keys by dimension + connection hash (Pass 206), and get()/peek() drop an entry the moment the block at those coordinates changes (currentBlockAt check). A chunk that unloads and reloads with the same block keeps the entry — but the entry is only 20 ticks stale before requestIfStale re-asks, and a reloaded chunk's block state is verified live on every get(). The stale-answer path (STATE_NONE + not looksSpoilable) refreshes every 200 ticks, which is the only case where a reloaded chunk could serve a stale verdict for a long time, and that verdict is 'nothing here' which is correct for an unloaded chunk. No code change.
[2026-09-10 00:06:59] · pass 977 · L3 cache · NO_BUG · ClientVirtualSpoilageAnchor eviction: MAX_ENTRIES=256 anchors, access-ordered LinkedHashMap with removeEldestEntry. Verified by code inspection: the eviction only fires when the map exceeds 256 entries AND the key is new, and the new entry is the one currently being asked for (the most useful to keep). No code change.

[2026-09-10 03:35:14] · pass 1031 · L4 hot-path TPS · FIXED · BlockSpoilageData.evictOldestRottenOrExpired uses posLong as tiebreaker for ALL non-ROTTEN entries, ignoring expirationTime — fresh blocks evicted before stale/expired ones. Fixed: eviction now prefers the entry with the SMALLEST expirationTime (closest to expiring). Legacy entries (expirationTime == -1) go to the end. Added regression test BlockSpoilageDataEvictionExpiryTest with two cases: closest-to-expiring evicted over smallest-position, and legacy entries go last. All tests pass (before/after verified).
[2026-09-10 04:52:54] · pass 1032 · L7 boundary arithmetic · FIXED · BlockSpoilageData.rescaleExpirations: (1) legacy entries multiplied elapsed by ratio instead of dividing — increasing the speed multiplier made blocks appear YOUNGER instead of older (ratio = old/new; elapsed must be divided by ratio, inverse of expirationTime scaling). (2) legacy format detection used legacyBirthTime >= 0, silently skipping negative birth times (born before world time 0). Fixed: elapsed / ratio, detection uses legacyBirthTime != -1, never-expires sentinel for legacy entries is Long.MIN_VALUE (Long.MAX_VALUE would still read as legacy and be re-scaled). 4 new tests in BlockSpoilageDataRescaleLegacyTest + updated BlockSpoilageDataRescaleTest/BlockEntryLegacyWinsTest. Full suite green (629 tests).

[2026-09-10 07:29:31] · pass 1036 · L7 boundary arithmetic · FIXED · (1) FoodSpoilageUtil.rescaleItemTimestamps stale branch missing ratio <= 0.0d guard — negative ratio caused stale items to appear fresh (remaining * ratio negative -> Math.max(1L, ...) -> huge positive). Added same guard as fresh branch. (2) BlockSpoilageData Long.MIN_VALUE sentinel treated as legacy birth time — currentTime - Long.MIN_VALUE overflowed to huge positive, making block appear ROTTEN instantly. Fixed: isLegacy checks now exclude Long.MIN_VALUE in getSpoilageState, getTicksUntilNextStage, toCompound, rescaleExpirations. Sentinel round-trips through NBT but is excluded from state computation. 3 new tests in LegacyNeverSentinelTest + RescaleNegativeRatioTest. Full suite green (635 tests).

16:10:03 · pass 1100 · L7 · FIXED · formatTime bit-packing key collision on large tick values · SpoilageEnhancedTranslations.java:125: old key (days << 42) | (hours << 21) | minutes allocated 21 bits each for hours (needs 5) and minutes (needs 6), leaving days only 22 bits — days >= 2^22 wrapped and collided (day 100 and day 100 + 2^22 produce the identical key (mathematically verified: (100 << 42) == ((100 + 2^22) << 42) in 64-bit long)), returning the wrong cached string. 22:34:23 · pass 1108 · L13 · NO_BUG · full-hunger stale math + offhand eat route · (1) FULL-HUNGER MATH driven live: ate fresh steak to foodLevel 20 (capped), then stale steak (nutrition 8, penalty 50%=4): 20 -> 16. Math is (current + nutrition [capped] - penalty), clamped correctly. Note: data modify entity foodLevel does NOT stick (FoodData is a live object, NBT view is separate) — full hunger must be reached by eating, not NBT. (2) OFFHAND ROUTE closed structurally from vanilla source: LivingEntity.completeUsingItem (LivingEntity.java:3565-3577) calls useItem.finishUsingItem(level, this) — the same method ItemStackMixin intercepts; startUsingItem accepts OFF_HAND (line 3507). 00:23:16 · pass 1111 · L13 · NO_BUG · villager trade refuses rotten food (guard exists, never driven) · Question: does a villager trade accept rotten food? Vanilla analysis: ItemCost.test (ItemCost.java:51-52) uses DataComponentExactPredicate, whose EMPTY form iterates zero expected components and always matches (DataComponentExactPredicate.java:77-86) — so vanilla WOULD accept rotten bread. The mod blocks it: SlotMixin.onCanTakeItems (SlotMixin.java:98-118) returns false from MerchantResultSlot.mayPickup when either buy slot holds ROTTEN spoilable food, and onTake stamps the trade result FRESH (SlotMixin.java:121-127). Stale food remains tradeable — consistent with the design (only ROTTEN is refused everywhere: crafting, furnace, brewing, hopper-into-furnace, trading). Guard never driven live (zero merchant mentions in 1110 passes of log+archive) and the injection point is unproven, but the guard logic (getWorstState==ROTTEN) is unit-tested and the pattern matches the proven ScreenHandler guards. Noted as unverified-injection, not a defect.

01:04:27 · pass 1113 · L7 boundary · FIXED · hand-edited config zero/negative default durations made all food instantly stale · SpoilageConfig.load validated spoilage_speed_multiplier (NaN/<=0/>100 clamps) but NOT default_fresh_duration_ticks / default_stale_duration_ticks. A hand-edited config with 0 or negative defaults flowed raw through every fallback path (getFreshDurationForItem, getStaleDurationForItem), so makeFresh computed expire = now + 0 and ALL food turned instantly stale, silently. Per-item overrides were already guarded (computeBaseDurations requires fresh > 0); the defaults were not. Fix: extracted the clamps into clampHandEditedValues() (package-private, testable) called from load(); durations <= 0 clamp to 24000, same value the fields initialise with. New ConfigHandEditedClampTest (4 tests, reflection pattern from ConfigCustomisationTest): zero/negative clamp, valid values survive, and the speed-multiplier clamps pinned so the extraction lost nothing. Suite 661/0/0 (657 baseline + 4 new).

Refuted by reading the code: BOTH getters route through the SAME applySpeedMultiplier helper (SpoilageConfig.java:613-616), which has the floor. There is no separate stale division path — no twin to fix. The setter also clamps to [0.01,100] (Pass 631) and the loader clamps the same range (pass 1113).
01:11:03 · pass 1115 · L7 boundary · NO_BUG · LEGACY_NEVER sentinel guard verified in control flow · Task: verify the :562 guard actually catches Long.MIN_VALUE before the :586 subtraction runs. Corrupt non-sentinel negatives (e.g. -9e18) read as ROTTEN — semantically correct (ancient = rotten) — and no overflow is reachable: currentTime + 9e18 stays under Long.MAX for any game time below 2.2e17 ticks (34,000 years). Comment is accurate; guard holds.
01:14:24 · pass 1116 · L7 boundary · NO_BUG · rottenTake Math.max(amount,0) guard: comments accurate, not claim drift · Task asked whether the redundant Math.max(amount,0) in rottenTake is claim drift. Kept for defensive consistency with extractWorstItems' (correct). Neither comment asserts anything false. Removing the guard saves one Math.max on a per-transfer path (not per-tick) and would churn two documented sites for nothing. No change.

01:46:36 · pass 1165 · L7 boundary · FIXED · ItemClientMixin + GuiGraphicsExtractorMixin overflow guard: huge staleDuration no longer wraps negative · Two client-side counting loops (tooltip in ItemClientMixin, inventory bar in GuiGraphicsExtractorMixin) computed exp + staleDuration with no overflow guard. staleDuration is unclamped by the config loader (same finding as pass 1164 — clampHandEditedValues touches only defaults and the speed multiplier), so a hand-edited stale value near Long.MAX_VALUE made the addition wrap negative. In ItemClientMixin the comparison currentTime >= (negative) read true and counted a fresh-expired item as ROTTEN in the tooltip though its stale window never ended. Fix: extracted classifyFreshExpiration to FoodSpoilageUtil with the guard (staleDuration > Long.MAX_VALUE - exp means STALE forever, never ROTTEN); ItemClientMixin calls it, GuiGraphicsExtractorMixin uses the inline equivalent. 08:41:30 · pass 1177 · L1 silent failure · FIXED · DynamicFoodBlockCacheSilentPoison: probe exceptions no longer poison either cache · getFoodDropIgnoringGrowth cached NO_FOOD_DROP even when the loot probe threw (world shutting down, block entity unloaded) — condemning the block until the 2048 cap cleared the map, i.e. forever in most packs. deriveRipeness had the same shape: a failed boolean-flag probe fell through to the age path, deriving a WRONG rule (Ripeness.always() for a flag plant) that ripenessOf then cached permanently. 09:12:40 · pass 1178 · L1 silent failure · REFUTED · AutoFoodDetectorUnboundComponents: safeComponents null does not hide modded food · Hypothesis: safeComponents returning null for unbound components makes detectFoodFactor skip modded CONSUMABLE-only items silently. 09:26:30 · pass 1179 · L1 silent failure · REFUTED · BlockSpoilageDataCorruptLoad: corrupt entry does not resurrect FRESH — it ages conservatively · Hypothesis: a corrupt Blocks entry (wrong tag type) loads as FRESH and resurrects a stale block. Traced the full path in source this pass: getCompoundOrEmpty on a non-CompoundTag value returns an EMPTY CompoundTag (CompoundTag.java:352-358, 'instanceof CompoundTag tag ? Optional.of(tag) : Optional.empty()') — no exception, no drop; the entry loads with State=0 (FRESH) and Expire=-1. But getSpoilageState with expirationTime=-1 hits 'currentTime >= entry.expirationTime' (BlockSpoilageData.java:608) on the FIRST ask, advancing FRESH->STALE (and to ROTTEN when staleDuration<=0) — the conservative direction, the opposite of a resurrection. The out-of-range State ordinal case was already fixed by pass 133 (bounded index, defaults FRESH, logged) and pinned by BlockSpoilageDataSaveLoadTest.outOfRangeStateOrdinalDefaultsToFreshInsteadOfDroppingEntry; the malformed-key case is pinned by malformedKeyDoesNotDropSiblingEntries (per-entry catch at line 129 isolates siblings — no whole-file quarantine happens); pass 245 already confirmed the logging is sufficient. No code change.

15:30:00 · pass 1184 · L13 observed · FIXED · Placed chests/barrels/shulker boxes were perfect freezers — ContainerAgingSweepMixin closes the gap · Live drive on the 79-mod pack: apple with fresh_expirations:[100L] placed via data modify into a chest (0,102,0), shulker box (0,100,0) and barrel (0,104,0) read back UNCHANGED after 35+ seconds on the pre-fix build — all three were freezers (chest control also frozen, which exposed the gap: the most common container in the game never aged contents; no prior pass had ever driven it — pass 500 marked shulker/ender chest BLOCKED 'GUI needed', data modify needs no GUI). Root cause: chests have no server ticker (ChestBlock.java:328 returns null server-side), so no per-block hook can reach them — every previously-fixed container (hopper, brewing stand, fridge, cooking pot, minecart, frame) had a tick to hook. Fix: ContainerAgingSweepMixin on MinecraftServer.tickServer walks loaded chunks via ChunkMapAccessor (new accessor on ChunkMap.updatingChunkMap) -> ChunkHolder.getTickingChunk() -> LevelChunk.getBlockEntities(), aging every Container BE. FIRST REVISION FAILED LIVE: gating the sweep on tickCount%20 and containers on shouldSkipAgingTick(pos,gameTime) never aged anything — tickCount starts at 0 on boot, gameTime continues from the save (1.68M, residue 13), so the sweep only ever ran at gameTime≡13 while containers needed gameTime≡(20-(x+z))%20; the two gates can never align for most positions. Diagnosed by adding per-container log lines that printed 6 containers found, 0 aged. Fix: phase-spread by CHUNK ((chunkX+chunkZ+gameTime)%20==0), age all containers in visited chunks — each container ages once per second regardless of position residue. Post-fix live readback: all three containers rotten_count:1. Non-food control (stone) gained no component. Suite 687/0/0. Committed 309ac1b.
18:45:00 · pass 1185 · L13 observed · FIXED · Ender chest contents never aged — PlayerEnderChestMixin closes the gap · Gap found by code reading and confirmed live: Player.tick calls inventory.tick() for the 36 main slots only (Player.java:448, Inventory.java:242-249); the ender chest is a separate PlayerEnderChestContainer that nothing ticks — EnderChestBlock.getTicker returns null server-side (EnderChestBlock.java:110), and the container lives on the player, not on a block entity, so pass 1184's ContainerAgingSweepMixin (which walks block entities) cannot reach it either. Fix: PlayerEnderChestMixin on Player.tick at RETURN, 20-tick cadence, probe-then-update shape (isSpoilable probe before any component access, updateSpoilage lazily stamps). Verified live through a NEW selftest scenario 'enderchest' (harness widened per 04_HUNTING.md: RCON cannot insert into a player's ender chest — vanilla refuses data modify on players — so the scenario drives the real path from the client: setblock ender chest, give apple with fresh_expirations:[now+200], debug use (real right-click), menuclick 54 QUICK_MOVE (real menu click; slot 54 = hotbar 0 in the 63-slot ChestMenu — first attempts at 37 and 36 clicked empty slots, fixed by reading ChestMenu.java:62-65 layout). Live readback: apple entered the ender chest with fresh_expirations:[1818189L] and 15s later read stale_expirations:[1851789L] — the fresh->stale transition happened INSIDE the ender chest. First scenario revision was inconclusive (hardcoded fresh_expirations:[600L] is an ABSOLUTE gameTime, 1.8M ticks in the past on this world, so the apple entered already rotten); fixed by building the expiration from the live clock now+200. Suite 687/0/0. Committed 260de7a.

20:30:00 · pass 1186 · L1 silent failure · FIXED · ContainerAgingSweepMixin had no per-container exception guard — one bad modded container would crash the server · The sweep (pass 1184) is the FIRST code that ever touches many containers (vanilla never ticks a chest), so a modded container whose getItem() throws would propagate through tickServer HEAD and crash the server every second. Added try/catch around ageContainer (log the container id, continue the sweep) and the same guard per-slot in PlayerEnderChestMixin (a corrupted ender-chest stack would otherwise kill Player.tick). Verified live on the 79-mod pack: chest still ages (rotten_count:1), zero containers skipped by the guard (grep 'skipped container' = 0). Also verified by source reading: the updatingChunkMap iteration is safe from concurrent modification (inject runs at HEAD of tickServer, before tickChildren -> chunkSource.tick mutates the map on the same thread; vanilla itself iterates it on the main thread at ChunkMap.java:353), and the double-pass in ageContainer is ~0.5us per food chest per second (27 CHM gets) — NO_BUG by arithmetic, not a hot path. Suite 687/0/0. Committed a6af363.
21:15:00 · pass 1187 · L14 foreign content · NO_BUG · Modded containers through the pass-1184 sweep: farmersdelight basket + oak cabinet age correctly; double-aging impossible by idempotency · Drove farmersdelight:oak_cabinet (0,106,0) and farmersdelight:basket (0,108,0) with tracked apples (fresh_expirations:[100L]) via data modify on the 79-mod pack: both read rotten_count:1 after 20s, identical to the vanilla chest control (0,102,0) driven in the same session. Slice: 2 of 2 farmersdelight container types (cabinet, basket); croptopia has NO block entities at all (checked the jar: no blockentity classes) so there is nothing to drive there. Double-aging hypothesis REFUTED by construction: updateSpoilage is a pure function of absolute gameTime (expiration lists hold absolute timestamps; the impl moves entries whose expiration has passed), so the hopper mixin (every tick) and the sweep (every 20 ticks) calling it on the same hopper is idempotent — confirmed live: hopper (0,110,0) with tracked apple aged to rotten_count:1 with both paths running, no anomaly. Uninhabited-chunk aging and offline ender-chest freezing both match the mod's design premise (vanilla does not tick offline players either) — documented in the two mixin javadocs. Suite 687/0/0. Committed 177c6b0.
22:30:00 · pass 1188 · L2 lifecycle · FIXED · enderchest selftest scenario was not idempotent across runs — leftovers from run N polluted run N+1's readback · The scenario (pass 1185) left its apple in the player's ender chest: vanilla has no /clear for ender chests and data modify on players is refused, so run N+1 read two apples and the readback was ambiguous (observed live: run 2 read a merged stack of 3 apples with 2 stale expirations). Fix: a drain step at the start (open the chest, QUICK_MOVE slot 0 back out), then clear the player AFTER the drain — the clear must come after because the drained apple lands in an unpredictable inventory slot and the give would merge into that stack instead of landing at hotbar 0 (observed live: apple landed at Slot 8b, menuclick 54 clicked an empty slot). New order: setblock+clear -> use+drain -> clear+give(now+200) -> use -> menuclick 54 insert. Verified live: run started with 3 leftover apples (cleared at 21:55:03), ended with EXACTLY ONE apple in the ender chest (EnderItems=[{apple, stale_expirations:[1894202L], count:1}]) — and that apple aged fresh->stale inside the chest during the run, re-confirming the pass-1185 mixin on the same drive. Suite 687/0/0. Committed b8e0c7b.

23:45:00 · pass 1189 · L13 observed · FIXED · Food inside a bundle (or nested shulker box) sitting in ANY container never aged · Three-layer defect found by driving a bundle with a tracked apple inside a chest: (1) updateSpoilage handled CONTAINER (shulker boxes) but not BUNDLE_CONTENTS — the bundle path was only called from ItemEntityMixin (dropped bundles) and ItemMixin (player inventory), so a bundle in a chest, hopper, brewing stand, minecart or ender chest never aged its contents; (2) the sweep's probe pass checked isSpoilable(stack) only — a bundle is not food, so the whole container was skipped as 'no food here'; (3) the update pass had the same isSpoilable filter, so even after the probe fix the bundle was skipped in the update loop. All three fixed: updateSpoilage now mirrors the CONTAINER branch for BUNDLE_CONTENTS; the probe flags stacks carrying food (BUNDLE_CONTENTS.items() / CONTAINER.nonEmptyItems() template scans, no ItemStack allocation); the update pass calls updateSpoilage on every non-empty stack of a flagged container (updateSpoilage's own guards no-op on stone: ~2 component lookups per stack per second, negligible). Verified live: bundle with fresh_expirations:[100L] apple inside a chest read rotten_count:1 after 25s (before the fix: unchanged after 25s, driven twice). Plain-apple control in the same chest still ages (probe fix did not break it). Nested shulker slice: RCON data modify strips the container component from block Items (vanilla sanitization), so that slice cannot be driven headless — mechanism identical to the bundle (same probe branch, same updateSpoilage CONTAINER branch proven live since pass 845); recorded as verified-by-mechanism with the limitation noted. No double-aging: ItemEntityMixin's bundle branch is else-if, ItemMixin's returns early. Suite 687/0/0. Committed 29ac449.
00:45:00 · pass 1190 · L12 claim drift · FIXED · PlayerEnderChestMixin javadoc claimed phase-spreading by entity id but the gate was bare tickCount % 20 · The javadoc (pass 1185) said 'phase-spread by the player's entity id (same phase-spreading as ItemEntityMixin)' — but the code was self.tickCount % 20 with no id. ItemEntityMixin uses (getId() + tickCount) % 20 (ItemEntityMixin.java:28). Every player's ender chest aged on the same tick boundary, and tickCount resets to 0 on entity load, so simultaneously-relogging players aligned too. Fixed the gate to (self.getId() + self.tickCount) % 20, matching the documented claim. Verified live: server launched clean (only the two expected client-class-skip warnings), the enderchest selftest scenario drove the real path end to end (drain removed the previous run's apple, give+insert placed exactly 1 apple), and the apple aged fresh->stale inside the chest (stale_expirations:[1932006L]) — the mixin with the new gate ages correctly. Also closed: the empty-server pause walk (NO_BUG — pauseWhenEmptySeconds() is 0 by default at MinecraftServer.java:2227, the waste only exists on opt-in servers, documented in the javadoc as a known non-issue) and the fridge design entry (NO_BUG — PLAYER_REPORTS.md entry is accurate, the sweep is consistent with it). 07:30:00 · pass 1197 · L1 silent failure · REFUTED · Four silent-failure hypotheses from the refill were all based on wrong premises · (1) ItemEntityMixin phase spread: the task claimed no entity-id spread, but ItemEntityMixin.java:28 uses (self.getId() + self.tickCount) % 20 since pass 100. (2) Bundle iteration concurrency: BundleContents is an immutable record in 26.2; contents.items() returns an unmodifiable list; all ticks run on the main server thread — CME impossible by construction. (3) ContainerAgingSweepMixin block-removal race: the sweep injects at tickServer HEAD, strictly before tickChildren processes any removals; a block broken in tick N is absent from tick N+1's sweep. (4) updateSpoilageDataLazy missing-tracker CHM cost: durations resolve once per call (freshDurationResolved flag), the loop reuses the value — a count=64 stack pays one lookup, not 64. All four tasks ticked REFUTED with the specific evidence. No source change.

[21:51:54] · pass 1208 · L3 cache · NO_BUG · DynamicFoodBlockCache eviction policy · The cap (2048) is a hint, not a guarantee — the code evicts one arbitrary entry (first in iteration order) when full, then inserts the new key (the one being probed, the most useful to keep). The concurrency race (two threads crossing the cap) can cause the map to slightly exceed 2048 or evict one extra entry — harmless for a cache. The comment already says 'evict one arbitrary entry so the new one has room.' The thrash scenario (evicting the hot entry every call) requires >2048 distinct probed block types, which is unreachable in practice. The log already answered this 4x (passes 715/745/765/780). 12:06 · pass 1283 · L13 observed behaviour · FIXED · Food in a bundle inside a bundle never aged (vanilla 26.2 allows bundle-in-bundle) · THREE defects found: (1) the food-carrying probe was depth 2, but vanilla 26.2 allows bundles inside bundles (BundleContents.java:32 BUNDLE_IN_BUNDLE_WEIGHT; canItemBeInBundle only requires canFitInsideContainerItems, BundleItem does not override) so Bundle->Bundle->Apple is reachable in pure vanilla — probe raised to depth 4 at both entry points; (2) updateBundleItemSpoilage's anySpoilable gate used bare isSpoilable, so a nested bundle (not spoilable itself) failed the gate and the function returned before aging anything — gate now uses the depth-aware carrier probe; (3) the update loop compared only the item's own SPOILAGE component before/after updateSpoilage, so a nested bundle whose CONTENTS changed but which has no SPOILAGE of its own left changed=false and the aged contents were never written back — now compares BUNDLE_CONTENTS too. Verified live both sides: pre-fix, tracked apple fresh_expirations:[1L] two bundles deep in a chest stayed fresh indefinitely while a DIRECT bundle in the same chest aged to rotten_count:1 (control); post-fix, the nested apple reads rotten_count:1. Pinned by bundleInBundleInBundleIsFlaggedByProbe (probe tests 4/0/0, suite 704/0/0). The old javadoc claim 'vanilla data components cannot cycle, so the limit only guards hypothetical foreign nesting' was wrong about depth — corrected with the vanilla citation.

16:38 · pass 1326 · L14 foreign content · NO_BUG · bclib/better-end plants — growthProperty coverage · Never asked. Static bytecode survey of the two jars (javap, no launch needed): bclib's BaseCropBlock and BasePlantWithAgeBlock both declare a static  IntegerProperty and register it in createBlockStateDefinition — so FoodSpoilageUtil.growthProperty(state) (which matches  exactly, then ) resolves them exactly as it does for vanilla crops, and deriveRipeness's age-based path covers them. better-end's PottableCropBlock (ShadowBerryBlock, BlossomBerryBlock, etc.) extends BushBlock and likewise carries its own  IntegerProperty with max 3. BoluxMushroomBlock extends BasePlantBlock with NO properties at all — no counter, no flag, so deriveRipeness returns Ripeness.always() and its drop (bolux_mushroom_cooked) is discovered through the loot table, the same path as a vanilla mushroom. No food-holding container BEs were found in either namespace (pass 1322 already probed that direction). The one shape worth naming: bclib's AGE is a SHARED static on BlockProperties, not per-block — growthProperty reads the state's own property list, so it cannot confuse two blocks that happen to share a property object, but a future pass adding a block with a non-/ counter would need the qualified-name fallback extended. No defect; the growthProperty probe covers this namespace by construction. No code change.

The task's "lettuce, tomato, salt" are from better_mcdonalds, not Ecologics — Ecologics' own foods are prickly_pear, coconut_slice, crab_meat, tropical_stew, cooked_prickly_pear, walnut, coconut_husk, coconut. No defect; the growthProperty and loot-table probes cover this namespace. No code change.

08:51 � pass 1334 � L17 � NO_BUG � time discontinuity � 12000-tick jump and backward /time set � RCON-driven with fresh, stale, and control apples in chests. Expirations are absolute timestamps (world.getGameTime() + duration). Time forward (sleep / time add 12000): currentTime increases, so fresh items with exp <= currentTime correctly transition to stale/rotten. Time backward (/time set 1000): currentTime decreases, but FoodSpoilageUtil.rescaleItemTimestamps (lines 322-324, 348-351) guards "Already expired � keep expired to prevent resurrection" � stale/rotten items stay stale/rotten; fresh items with exp > currentTime simply see their remaining time increase (appear frozen/younger). No negative remaining times, no items coming out already rotten from a jump, no resurrection. The control (vanilla apple stamped on load) behaved identically. No code change.
